(61c)

The analytic expressions corresponding to Eq. (
61
)
are in Eq. (
20
).

Appendix B
Symmetries of the effective interaction vertices

In this section we derive Eq. (
21
) by weak induction
using (
18
) as base case
n=1
and the induction step in (
20
).
One readily verifies that (
18
) satisfies (
21
).
We first show Eq. (
21a
).
Changing the sign of the dummy variable
k_{i}
in Eq. (
20
) by substituting

k_{i}\mapsto-k_{i}
effectively changes every
E
into
G
and vice versa, Eqs. (
11a
) and (
11b
), and leaves
D
unchanged, Eq. (
11d
). Further, under
k_{i}\mapsto-k_{i}
every dashed potential becomes undashed and vice versa, so that Eq. (
20
) gives

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{n+1}(k_{j})=

\displaystyle(-k_{j})\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}\frac{\nu\xi^{-2}(k_{j}+k_{i})}{(k_{j}+k_{i})^{2}+\xi^{-2}}DD^{\prime}\Big\{\begin{aligned} &HH(\Phi_{n}^{\prime}+\Phi_{n})+GH(\Psi_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Psi}_{n})\\
+&HG^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}^{\prime}+\Psi_{n})+GG^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Phi}_{n})\Big\}\ .\end{aligned}

(62)

Evaluating
\widetilde{\Phi}_{n+1}
at
-k_{j}
and using Eq. (
21
)
we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{n+1}(-k_{j})=

\displaystyle k_{j}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}\frac{\nu\xi^{-2}(-k_{j}+k_{i})}{(-k_{j}+k_{i})^{2}+\xi^{-2}}DD^{\prime}\Big\{\begin{aligned} &HH(\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Phi}_{n})+GH(\Psi_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Psi}_{n})\\
+&HG^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}^{\prime}+\Psi_{n})+GG^{\prime}(\Phi_{n}^{\prime}+\Phi_{n})\Big\}\ ,\end{aligned}

(63)

which is equal to the right-hand side of Eq. (
20
).
It follows that

\widetilde{\Phi}_{n+1}(-k_{j})=\Phi_{n+1}(k_{j})\ .

(64)

The final step to demonstrate (
21a
) from Eq. (
64
)
is to show that
\widetilde{\Phi}_{n+1}(k_{j})
is even in
k_{j}
.
By physical reasoning this follows from the equal-species effective potential
\Phi(x)

being even in
x
, but we choose to demonstrate this on mathematical grounds.
Changing the sign of the dummy variables in Eq. (
20
),
\omega^{\prime}\mapsto-\omega^{\prime}
and
k_{i}\mapsto-k_{i}
, effectively replaces every
D,G,E
by
D^{\prime},G^{\prime},E^{\prime}
and vice versa, while leaving
H
unchanged. Similarly, every effective vertex
gets dashed if it appears undashed, and vice versa. It follows that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{n+1}(k_{j})=

\displaystyle(-k_{j})\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}\frac{\nu\xi^{-2}(k_{j}+k_{i})}{(k_{j}+k_{i})^{2}+\xi^{-2}}DD^{\prime}\Big\{\begin{aligned} &HH(\Phi_{n}^{\prime}+\Phi_{n})+E^{\prime}H(\Psi_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Psi}_{n})\\
+&HE(\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}^{\prime}+\Psi_{n})+E^{\prime}E(\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Phi}_{n})\Big\}\end{aligned}

(65)

which, evaluated at
-k_{j}
, gives
the right-hand side of Eq. (
20
).
It follows that
\widetilde{\Phi}_{n+1}
is even in
k_{j}

and, together with Eq. (
64
),
so is
\Phi_{n+1}
. In summary, we have

\Phi_{n+1}(-k_{j})=\widetilde{\Phi}_{n+1}(k_{j})=\widetilde{\Phi}_{n+1}(-k_{j})=\Phi_{n+1}(k_{j})

(66)

for all
n
and thus Eq. (
21a
).

As a final property of
\Phi(k_{j})
, we show that it is real
for
k_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
, which, again, follows from physical reasoning that
\Phi(x)
is real and even.
To show this explicitly we use that
the mapping
\omega^{\prime}\mapsto-\omega^{\prime}
and
k_{i}\mapsto-k_{i}
, which exchanges dashes.
This operation is identical to taking the complex conjugate because every term in
G,E,H,D
that is odd in
k_{j}
is imaginary and every term that is even in
k_{j}
is real. The same applies to
\omega
. As a result, for example,
D^{*}=D^{\prime}
, and therefore
(DD^{\prime})^{*}=DD^{\prime}

is bound to be real. Assuming that

\displaystyle\Phi^{*}_{n}(k_{j})=

\displaystyle\Phi_{n}(-k_{j})\ ,

(67a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{n}(k_{j})=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(-k_{j})\ ,

(67b)

\displaystyle\Psi^{*}_{n}(k_{j})=

\displaystyle\Psi_{n}(-k_{j})\ ,

(67c)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}^{*}_{n}(k_{j})=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(-k_{j})\ ,

(67d)

which holds for the base case (
18
),
gives for Eq. (
20
)

\displaystyle\Phi^{*}_{n+1}(k_{j})=

\displaystyle(-k_{j})\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}\frac{\nu\xi^{-2}(k_{j}-k_{i})}{(k_{j}-k_{i})^{2}+\xi^{-2}}DD^{\prime}\Big\{\begin{aligned} &GG^{\prime}(\Phi_{n}^{\prime}+\Phi_{n})+HG(\Psi_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Psi}_{n})\\
+&G^{\prime}H(\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}^{\prime}+\Psi_{n})+HH(\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Phi}_{n})\Big\}\ ,\end{aligned}

(68)

whose right-hand side is
identical to Eq. (
20
). Thus

\Phi^{*}_{n+1}(k_{j})=\Phi_{n+1}(k_{j})\ ,

(69)

which will be combined below with the corresponding property of
\Psi_{n}
,
Eqs. (
74
), to confirm Eqs. (
67
) for all
n
.
In summary, the effective vertex
\Phi_{n}(k_{j})=\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}(k_{j})
is even and real.

To show Eq. (
21b
), we repeat a similar argument for
\Psi_{n}
,
which is slightly more intricate, because
\Psi_{n}
contains a real and an imaginary part.
First, replacing
k_{i}\mapsto-k_{i}
in Eq. (
20
) and evaluating at
-k_{j}
gives

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{n+1}(-k_{j})=

\displaystyle k_{j}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}\frac{\nu\xi^{-2}(-k_{j}+k_{i})}{(-k_{j}+k_{i})^{2}+\xi^{-2}}DD^{\prime}\Big\{\begin{aligned} &EH(\Phi_{n}^{\prime}+\Phi_{n})+HH(\Psi_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Psi}_{n})\\
+&EG^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}^{\prime}+\Psi_{n})+HG^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Phi}_{n})\Big\}\ ,\end{aligned}

(70)

which is equal to
\Psi_{n+1}(k_{j})
in (
20
), provided Eq. (
21
).
It follows that,

\widetilde{\Psi}_{n+1}(-k_{j})=\Psi_{n+1}(k_{j})\ ,

(71)

confirming Eq. (
21b
) for all
n
.
The complex conjugate of
\Psi_{n+1}
in (
20
) is,
assuming Eq. (
67
),

\displaystyle\Psi^{*}_{n+1}(k_{j})=

\displaystyle(-k_{j})\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}\frac{\nu\xi^{-2}(k_{j}-k_{i})}{(k_{j}-k_{i})^{2}+\xi^{-2}}D^{\prime}D\Big\{\begin{aligned} &HG(\Phi_{n}^{\prime}+\Phi_{n})+E^{\prime}G(\Psi_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Psi}_{n})\\
+&HH(\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}^{\prime}+\Psi_{n})+E^{\prime}H(\widetilde{\Phi}_{n}^{\prime}+\widetilde{\Phi}_{n})\Big\}\ ,\end{aligned}

(72)

which is exactly Eq. (
20
). Therefore,

\Psi^{*}_{n+1}(k_{j})=\widetilde{\Psi}_{n+1}(k_{j})

(73)

and combined with Eq. (
71
), we have

\displaystyle\Psi^{*}_{n+1}(k_{j})=

\displaystyle\Psi_{n+1}(-k_{j})

(74a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}^{*}_{n+1}(k_{j})=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{n+1}(-k_{j})\ ,

(74b)

together with Eq. (
69
)
confirming Eqs. (
67
)
for arbitrary
n

and confirming the physical picture that
\Phi(x)
and
\Psi(x)
are real effective pair-potentials.

Appendix C
Evaluation of loop integrals over
\omega

The loop integrals over
\omega
in Eq. (
27
) are evaluated
using Eqs. (
29
) and (
30
).
We use the following parametrisation so as to systematically define
the iterative scheme.
For
P_{n+1}
in Eq. (
27a
), we need,

\displaystyle\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}2DD^{\prime}(GG^{\prime}+HH)k_{i}^{2}=

\displaystyle f_{P}(k_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi_{P0}+\frac{\varphi_{P1}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\varphi_{P2}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathsf{D}_{x}}-\frac{\gamma\mathsf{w}^{2}}{\mathsf{D}_{x}^{2}}\frac{1}{\gamma\mathsf{D}_{x}+\mathsf{w}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\mathsf{D}_{x}}\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\gamma\mathsf{D}_{x}+\mathsf{w}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\ ,

(75a)

\displaystyle\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}2DD^{\prime}(HG^{\prime}+GH)k_{i}^{2}=

\displaystyle f_{Q}(k_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi_{Q0}+\frac{\varphi_{Q1}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\varphi_{Q2}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{\mathsf{D}_{x}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\ ,

(75b)

\displaystyle\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}2DD^{\prime}(HG^{\prime}-GH)\mathring{\imath}k_{i}=

\displaystyle f_{R}(k_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi_{R0}+\frac{\varphi_{R1}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\varphi_{R2}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathsf{w}\gamma}{\mathsf{D}_{x}}\frac{1}{\gamma\mathsf{D}_{x}+\mathsf{w}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{w}\gamma}{\mathsf{D}_{x}}\frac{1}{\gamma\mathsf{D}_{x}+\mathsf{w}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\ .

(75c)

Similarly, for
Q_{n+1}
in Eq. (
27
) we need,

\displaystyle\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}DD^{\prime}(HG^{\prime}+GH+EH+HE^{\prime})k_{i}^{2}=

\displaystyle g_{P}(k_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma_{P0}+\frac{\gamma_{P1}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\gamma_{P2}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{\mathsf{D}_{x}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\ ,

(76a)

\displaystyle\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}DD^{\prime}(EG^{\prime}+HH+HH+GE^{\prime})k_{i}^{2}=

\displaystyle g_{Q}(k_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma_{Q0}+\frac{\gamma_{Q1}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\gamma_{Q2}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathsf{D}_{x}}-\frac{\gamma\mathsf{D}_{x}+\mathsf{w}^{2}}{\mathsf{D}_{x}^{3}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\ ,

(76b)

\displaystyle\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}DD^{\prime}(EG^{\prime}+HH-HH-GE^{\prime})\mathring{\imath}k_{i}=

\displaystyle g_{R}(k_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma_{R0}+\frac{\gamma_{R1}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\gamma_{R2}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-\mathsf{w}}{\mathsf{D}_{x}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\ .

(76c)

Further, for
R_{n+1}
in Eq. (
27
) we need,

\displaystyle\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}DD^{\prime}(HG^{\prime}-GH+EH-HE^{\prime})k_{i}^{2}=

\displaystyle\mathring{\imath}k_{i}h_{P}(k_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathring{\imath}k_{i}\bigg(\eta_{P0}+\frac{\eta_{P1}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\eta_{P2}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathring{\imath}k_{i}\bigg(\frac{\gamma\mathsf{w}}{\mathsf{D}_{x}(\gamma\mathsf{D}_{x}+\mathsf{w}^{2})}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}-\frac{\gamma\mathsf{w}}{\mathsf{D}_{x}(\gamma\mathsf{D}_{x}+\mathsf{w}^{2})}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\bigg)\ ,

(77a)

\displaystyle\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}DD^{\prime}(EG^{\prime}-HH+HH-GE^{\prime})k_{i}^{2}=

\displaystyle\mathring{\imath}k_{i}h_{Q}(k_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathring{\imath}k_{i}\bigg(\eta_{Q0}+\frac{\eta_{Q1}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\eta_{Q2}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathring{\imath}k_{i}\bigg(\frac{\mathsf{w}}{\mathsf{D}_{x}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\bigg)\ ,

(77b)

\displaystyle\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-7.0mu\mathchar 22\omega^{\prime}\,}DD^{\prime}(EG^{\prime}-HH-HH+GE^{\prime})\mathring{\imath}k_{i}=

\displaystyle\mathring{\imath}k_{i}h_{R}(k_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathring{\imath}k_{i}\bigg(\eta_{R0}+\frac{\eta_{R1}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\eta_{R2}}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathring{\imath}k_{i}\bigg(\frac{\gamma}{\gamma\mathsf{D}_{x}+\mathsf{w}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{w}^{2}/\mathsf{D}_{x}}{\gamma\mathsf{D}_{x}+\mathsf{w}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\bigg)\ .

(77c)

Appendix D
Evaluation of loop sums over
k_{j}
as infinite series

Figure 7:
Square contour
C_{N}
for
N=4
and simple poles of
g(z)=\cot{\left({Lz}/{2}\right)}f(z)
,
Eq. (
79
).
The poles of
\cot{\left({Lz}/{2}\right)}
are
z=k_{i}
(black), and the poles of

f(z)
are
z=\pm\mathring{\imath}\alpha
(red) and
z=k_{j}\pm\mathring{\imath}\xi^{-1}
(blue) with
j=2
,
Eqs. (
83
) and (
87
).

The infinite series in Eqs. (
42
) have the form

\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}f(k_{i})=\frac{1}{L}\left(\sum_{i=-\infty}^{\infty}f(k_{i})-f(0)\right)\ ,

(78)

with wavenumber
k_{i}=2\pi i/L
, and
are evaluated by applying
Cauchy’s residue theorem
Spiegel et al. 2009
;
Marsden and Hoffman 1973
.
We consider the contour integral

\oint_{C_{N}}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}g(z)
of

g(z)=\cot{\left(\frac{Lz}{2}\right)}f(z)

(79)

where the contour
C_{N}
is a square defined by the four vertices
(\pm R_{N},\pm R_{N})

with
R_{N}=2\pi(N+1/2)/L

and
N\in\mathbb{N}

and
f
is the summand in Eqs. (
42
), Fig.
7
.
As
|\cot(Lz/2)|<2
along the contour
C_{N}
with
N\geq 1
and both summands in Eqs. (
42
) obey

|f(z)|\leq 1/z^{2}
, the integrand along the contour of length
\sim N
is suitably bounded by
\sim N^{-2}
,
namely
\left|g(z)\right|<{2}/{|z|^{2}}
for
z\in C_{N}
, so that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\oint_{C_{N}}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}g(z)=0\ .

(80)

This integrand has simple poles at the singularities of
\cot{\left({Lz}/{2}\right)}

and
f(z)
inside
C_{N}
, which are
z=k_{i}
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
with
k_{i}\in\mathbb{R}

and
\{z_{1},z_{2},\ldots\}
with
z_{i}\notin\mathbb{R}
respectively, to be determined below.
According to Cauchy’s residue theorem
Spiegel et al. 2009
;
Marsden and Hoffman 1973
,
this contour integral equals the sum of the residues
at the poles enclosed by the contour,

\oint_{C_{N}}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}{\!\mathrm{d}z\,}\cot{\left(\frac{Lz}{2}\right)}f(z)=2\pi\mathring{\imath}\left[\sum_{i=-N}^{N}f(k_{i})\operatorname{Res}_{z=k_{i}}\cot(z)+\sum_{i}\cot(z_{i})\operatorname{Res}_{z=z_{i}}f(z)\right]\ .

(81)

As the residues of the poles of
\cot{\left({Lz}/{2}\right)}
at
z=k_{i}
for
i\in\mathbb{Z}
are
2/L
,
combining Eqs. (
80
) and (
81
) implies

\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=-\infty}^{\infty}f(k_{i})=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i}\cot(z_{i})\operatorname{Res}_{z=z_{i}}f(z)\ ,

(82)

thus reducing an infinite sum to that over a small, finite number of terms.
To evaluate the series in Eq. (
42a
), we choose

f(z)=\frac{k_{j}-z}{(k_{j}-z)^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\frac{1}{z^{2}+\alpha^{2}}\ ,

(83)

which has simple poles of
f(z)
are
z=\pm\mathring{\imath}\alpha
and
z=k_{j}\pm\mathring{\imath}\xi^{-1}
, Fig.
7
, with
the following residues,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z=\mathring{\imath}\alpha}f(z)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\mathring{\imath}\alpha}\frac{k_{j}-\mathring{\imath}\alpha}{(k_{j}-\mathring{\imath}\alpha)^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\ ,

(84a)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z=-\mathring{\imath}\alpha}f(z)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\mathring{\imath}\alpha}\frac{k_{j}+\mathring{\imath}\alpha}{(k_{j}+\mathring{\imath}\alpha)^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\ ,

(84b)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z=k_{j}+\mathring{\imath}\xi^{-1}}f(z)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{(k_{j}+\mathring{\imath}\xi^{-1})^{2}+\alpha^{2}}\ ,

(84c)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z=k_{j}-\mathring{\imath}\xi^{-1}}f(z)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{(k_{j}-\mathring{\imath}\xi^{-1})^{2}+\alpha^{2}}\ .

(84d)

Using the identity

\cot{\left(\frac{L(k_{j}+\mathring{\imath}\alpha)}{2}\right)}=\mathring{\imath}\frac{e^{-L\alpha}+1}{e^{-L\alpha}-1}\ ,

(85)

we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=-\infty}^{\infty}f(k_{i})=

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathring{\imath}}{2}\begin{aligned} &\left(\frac{1}{2\alpha\mathring{\imath}}\frac{e^{-L\alpha}+1}{e^{-L\alpha}-1}\frac{k_{j}-\mathring{\imath}\alpha}{(k_{j}-\mathring{\imath}\alpha)^{2}+\xi^{-2}}-\frac{1}{2\alpha\mathring{\imath}}\frac{e^{L\alpha}+1}{e^{L\alpha}-1}\frac{k_{j}+\mathring{\imath}\alpha}{(k_{j}+\mathring{\imath}\alpha)^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\right.\\
&\left.-\frac{1}{2}\frac{e^{-L\xi^{-1}}+1}{e^{-L\xi^{-1}}-1}\frac{1}{(k_{j}+\mathring{\imath}\xi^{-1})^{2}+\alpha^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{e^{L/\xi}+1}{e^{L/\xi}-1}\frac{1}{(k_{j}-\mathring{\imath}\xi^{-1})^{2}+\alpha^{2}}\right)\end{aligned}

(86a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k_{j}}{2\alpha}\left(\frac{A(\alpha)}{k_{j}^{2}+(\alpha+\xi^{-1})^{2}}+\frac{B(\alpha)}{k_{j}^{2}+(\alpha-\xi^{-1})^{2}}\right)\ ,

(86b)

with coefficients
A(\alpha)
and
B(\alpha)
defined in Eqs. (
43
).
We obtain Eq. (
42a
) by subtracting the zero-mode,

f(0)={k_{j}}/\left(\alpha^{2}{(k_{j}^{2}+\xi^{-2})}\right)
, which produces its final term algebraic in
L
.
The evaluation of the infinite series in Eq. (
42b
) follows the same steps as above, giving

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=-\infty}^{\infty}f(k_{i})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{k_{j}-k_{i}}{(k_{j}-k_{i})^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\frac{k_{i}}{k_{i}^{2}+\alpha^{2}}

(87a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}{\left(\frac{A(\alpha)(\alpha+\xi^{-1})}{k_{j}^{2}+(\alpha+\xi^{-1})^{2}}+\frac{B(\alpha)(\alpha-\xi^{-1})}{k_{j}^{2}+(\alpha-\xi^{-1})^{2}}\right)}\ .

(87b)

In this case, removing the zero-mode leaves the sum unchanged because
f(0)=0
.

Appendix E
Iteration of the functions
P
,
Q
and
R

The sets of poles of each type,
p^{(0)}_{n,m}
,
p^{(1)}_{n,m}
and
p^{(2)}_{n,m}
, listed in Eq. (
45
)
have indices

m\in[1,M_{n}^{(0)}]
and
m\in[-M_{n}^{(i)},M_{n}^{(i)}]
, with
M_{n}^{(0)}=n
and
M_{n}^{(i)}=n-1
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Henceforth, denoting any pole in the set of
M_{n}=M_{n}^{(0)}+2M_{n}^{(1)}+2M_{n}^{(2)}+2

poles as
p_{n,m}
, we use the shorthand notation

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M_{n}}f(p_{n,m})=\sum_{{m}=1}^{M^{(0)}_{n}}f(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)})+\sum_{m=-M^{(1)}_{n}}^{M^{(1)}_{n}}f(p^{(1)}_{n,m})+\sum_{m=-M^{(2)}_{n}}^{M^{(2)}_{n}}f(p^{(2)}_{n,m})

(88)

for arbitrary
f
.
For better readability, we introduce the bracket notation

\displaystyle[\varphi]_{n,m}

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} \bigg[&\Big(\varphi_{P0}+\frac{\varphi_{P1}}{a_{1}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}+\frac{\varphi_{P2}}{a_{2}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}\Big)\pi_{n,m}\\
+&\Big(\varphi_{Q0}+\frac{\varphi_{Q1}}{a_{1}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}+\frac{\varphi_{Q2}}{a_{2}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}\Big)\zeta_{n,m}\\
+&\Big(\varphi_{R0}+\frac{\varphi_{R1}}{a_{1}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}+\frac{\varphi_{R2}}{a_{2}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}\Big)\rho_{n,m}\bigg]\ ,\end{aligned}

(89a)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{1}|_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M_{n}}\bigg[\frac{\varphi_{P1}}{a_{1}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}\pi_{n,m}+\frac{\varphi_{Q1}}{a_{1}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}\zeta_{n,m}+\frac{\varphi_{R1}}{a_{1}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}\rho_{n,m}\bigg]\ ,

(89b)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{2}|_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M_{n}}\bigg[\frac{\varphi_{P2}}{a_{2}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}\pi_{n,m}+\frac{\varphi_{Q2}}{a_{2}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}\zeta_{n,m}+\frac{\varphi_{R2}}{a_{2}^{2}-p_{n,m}^{2}}\rho_{n,m}\bigg]\ .

(89c)

Using the parametrisation in Eqs. (
37
) and (
89
),
which assumes simple poles
\pm\mathring{\imath}p^{(i)}_{n,m}
,
and the identity (
41
),
the next order
P_{n+1}(k_{j})
of
P_{n}
in Eq. (
IV.3
) reads

\displaystyle P_{n+1}(k_{j})k_{j}^{2}=(-k_{j})\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}(k_{j}-k_{i})\frac{(\nu\xi^{-2})^{n+1}}{(k_{j}-k_{i})^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\Bigg\{\sum_{m=1}^{M_{n}}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+p_{n,m}^{2}}[\varphi]_{n,m}-\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}|\varphi_{1}|_{n}-\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}|\varphi_{2}|_{n}\Bigg\}\ .

(90)

Using now the Matsubara sum in Eq. (
42a
),
the loop sum over
k_{i}
produces,

\displaystyle P_{n+1}(k_{j})=-(\nu\xi^{-2})^{n+1}\Bigg\{

\displaystyle-|\varphi_{1}|_{n}\frac{1}{2a_{1}}{\left(\frac{A(a_{1})}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{1}+\xi^{-1})^{2}}+\frac{B(a_{1})}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{1}-\xi^{-1})^{2}}-\frac{2}{La_{1}}\frac{1}{k_{j}^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\right)}

(91a)

\displaystyle-|\varphi_{2}|_{n}\frac{1}{2a_{2}}{\left(\frac{A(a_{2})}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{2}+\xi^{-1})^{2}}+\frac{B(a_{2})}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{2}-\xi^{-1})^{2}}-\frac{2}{La_{2}}\frac{1}{k_{j}^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\right)}

(91b)

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=1}^{M_{n}}[\varphi]_{n,m}\frac{1}{2p_{n,m}}{\left(\frac{A(p_{n,m})}{k_{j}^{2}+(p_{n,m}+\xi^{-1})^{2}}+\frac{B(p_{n,m})}{k_{j}^{2}+(p_{n,m}-\xi^{-1})^{2}}-\frac{2}{Lp_{n,m}}\frac{1}{k_{j}^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\right)}\Bigg\}\ .

(91c)

The process for
Q_{n}
, Eq. (
22b
), is similar.
Using Eqs. (
37
) and (
41
),

Q_{n+1}(k_{j})
in Eq. (
IV.3
) reads

\displaystyle Q_{n+1}k_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle=(-k_{j})\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}(k_{j}-k_{i})\frac{(\nu\xi^{-2})^{n+1}}{(k_{j}-k_{i})^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\Bigg\{\sum_{m=1}^{M_{n}}[\gamma]_{n,m}\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+p_{n,m}^{2}}-\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{1}^{2}}|\gamma_{1}|_{n}-\frac{1}{k_{i}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}|\gamma_{2}|_{n}\Bigg\}\ ,

(92)

where the notation
[\gamma]_{n,m}
,
|\gamma_{1}|_{n}
and
|\gamma_{2}|_{n}

follow the same pattern as the notation in Eq. (
89
)
with
\varphi
-coefficients replaced by
\gamma
-coefficients.
Using the sum in Eq. (
42a
),

\displaystyle Q_{n+1}(k_{j})=-(\nu\xi^{-2})^{n+1}\Bigg\{

\displaystyle-|\gamma_{1}|_{n}\frac{1}{2a_{1}}{\left(\frac{A(a_{1})}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{1}+\xi^{-1})^{2}}+\frac{B(a_{1})}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{1}-\xi^{-1})^{2}}-\frac{2}{La_{1}}\frac{1}{k_{j}^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\right)}

(93a)

\displaystyle-|\gamma_{2}|_{n}\frac{1}{2a_{2}}{\left(\frac{A(a_{2})}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{2}+\xi^{-1})^{2}}+\frac{B(a_{2})}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{2}-\xi^{-1})^{2}}-\frac{2}{La_{2}}\frac{1}{k_{j}^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\right)}

(93b)

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=1}^{M_{n}}[\gamma]_{n,m}\frac{1}{2p_{n,m}}{\left(\frac{A(p_{n,m})}{k_{j}^{2}+(p_{n,m}+\xi^{-1})^{2}}+\frac{B(p_{n,m})}{k_{j}^{2}+(p_{n,m}-\xi^{-1})^{2}}-\frac{2}{Lp_{n,m}}\frac{1}{k_{j}^{2}+\xi^{-2}}\right)}\Bigg\}\ .

(93c)

Finally, we calculate the loop sum in Eq. (
IV.3
) using
Eq. (
42b
), which gives

\displaystyle R_{n+1}(k_{j})=(\nu\xi^{-2})^{n+1}\Bigg\{

\displaystyle-|\eta|_{1}\frac{1}{2}{\left(A(a_{1})\frac{a_{1}+\xi^{-1}}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{1}+\xi^{-1})^{2}}+B(a_{1})\frac{a_{1}-\xi^{-1}}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{1}-\xi^{-1})^{2}}\right)}

(94a)

\displaystyle-|\eta|_{2}\frac{1}{2}{\left(A(a_{2})\frac{a_{2}+\xi^{-1}}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{2}+\xi^{-1})^{2}}+B(a_{2})\frac{a_{2}-\xi^{-1}}{k_{j}^{2}+(a_{2}-\xi^{-1})^{2}}\right)}

(94b)

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=1}^{M_{n}}[\eta]_{n,m}\frac{1}{2}{\left(A(p_{n,m})\frac{p_{n,m}+\xi^{-1}}{k_{j}^{2}+(p_{n,m}+\xi^{-1})^{2}}+B(p_{n,m})\frac{p_{n,m}-\xi^{-1}}{k_{j}^{2}+(p_{n,m}-\xi^{-1})^{2}}\right)}\Bigg\}\ ,

(94c)

where the notation
[\eta]_{n,m}
,
|\eta_{1}|_{n}
and
|\eta_{2}|_{n}

is obtained from Eq. (
89
)
by replacing
\varphi
-coefficients by
\eta
-coefficients.

Appendix F
Iteration of the coefficients
\pi
,
\zeta
and
\rho

Following an algorithmic approach, in this section we write explicitly
how the residue of each simple pole at order
n
contributes additively to a particular amplitude and thus
the residues of other simple poles at
order
n+1
in the perturbation theory.
This is done by identifying the coefficients in Eqs. (
91
), (
93
)
and (
94
) as contributions to
\pi_{n+1,m}
,
\zeta_{n+1,m}
and
\rho_{n+1,m}

for suitable
m
according to the parametrisation in Eq. (
37
).
As a matter of pragmatism, we ignore all
a_{i}
-type poles at
m\leq 0
, Fig.
1
(right),
as they are exponentially suppressed.
We first introduce the following bracket notation, similar to Eq. (
89a
),

\displaystyle[\varphi]^{(i)}_{n,m}=\bigg[

\displaystyle\Big(\varphi_{P0}+\frac{\varphi_{P1}}{a_{1}^{2}-(p^{(i)}_{n,m})^{2}}+\frac{\varphi_{P2}}{a_{2}^{2}-(p^{(i)}_{n,m})^{2}}\Big)\pi^{(i)}_{n,m}

(95)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(\varphi_{Q0}+\frac{\varphi_{Q1}}{a_{1}^{2}-(p^{(i)}_{n,m})^{2}}+\frac{\varphi_{Q2}}{a_{2}^{2}-(p^{(i)}_{n,m})^{2}}\Big)\zeta^{(i)}_{n,m}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(\varphi_{R0}+\frac{\varphi_{R1}}{a_{1}^{2}-(p^{(i)}_{n,m})^{2}}+\frac{\varphi_{R2}}{a_{2}^{2}-(p^{(i)}_{n,m})^{2}}\Big)\rho^{(i)}_{n,m}\bigg]\ .

F.1
Contributions to
\pi_{n+1,m}

Contributions to
\pi_{n+1,m}
are read off from Eq. (
91
) as follows.
For
\xi
-type poles
p^{(0)}_{n,m}
, the
A
-term in
Eq. (
91c
)
produces an up-shifted pole of
\xi
-type, the
B
-term a down-shifted
\xi
-type, but only if
m>1
.
Similarly, for
a_{1}
-type poles
p^{(1)}_{n,m}
, the
A
-term produces an up-shifted
a_{1}
-type and the
B
-term a downshifted
a_{1}
-type without exception.
The same happens for
a_{2}
-type poles
p^{(2)}_{n,m}
.
Within
Eq. (
91c
), the last term always contributes a
\xi
-type pole
p^{(0)}_{n+1,1}
.
In summary, from
Eq. (
91c
) we deduce the following contributions to
\pi_{n+1,m}
,

\displaystyle-\frac{A(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)})}{2p_{n,{m}}^{(0)}}[\varphi]_{n,{m}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &{\pi}^{(0)}_{n+1,{m}+1}&\text{for }{m}=1,\ldots,M^{(0)}_{n}\end{aligned}

(96a)

\displaystyle-\frac{B(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)})}{2p_{n,{m}}^{(0)}}[\varphi]_{n,{m}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &{\pi}^{(0)}_{n+1,{m}-1}&\text{for }{m}=2,\ldots,M^{(0)}_{n}\end{aligned}

(96b)

\displaystyle-\frac{A(p^{(1)}_{n,m})}{2p^{(1)}_{n,m}}[\varphi]_{n,m}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &\pi^{(1)}_{n+1,m+1}&\text{for }m=-M^{(1)}_{n},\ldots,M^{(1)}_{n}\end{aligned}

(96c)

\displaystyle-\frac{B(p^{(1)}_{n,m})}{2p^{(1)}_{n,m}}[\varphi]_{n,m}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &\pi^{(1)}_{n+1,m-1}&\text{for }m=-M^{(1)}_{n},\ldots,M^{(1)}_{n}\end{aligned}

(96d)

\displaystyle-\frac{A(p^{(2)}_{n,m})}{2p^{(2)}_{n,m}}[\varphi]_{n,m}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &\pi^{(2)}_{n+1,m+1}&\text{for }m=-M^{(2)}_{n},\ldots,M^{(2)}_{n}\end{aligned}

(96e)

\displaystyle-\frac{B(p^{(2)}_{n,m})}{2p^{(2)}_{n,m}}[\varphi]_{n,m}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &\pi^{(2)}_{n+1,m-1}&\text{for }m=-M^{(2)}_{n},\ldots,M^{(2)}_{n}\end{aligned}

(96f)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{m=1}^{M_{n}}\frac{1}{(p_{n,m})^{2}}[\varphi]_{n,m}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\pi}^{(0)}_{n+1,1}\ .

(96g)

Contributions from Eq. (
91a
) are to coefficients

\pi^{(1)}_{n+1,\pm 1}
and
\pi^{(0)}_{n+1,1}
,

\displaystyle\frac{A(a_{1})}{2a_{1}}|\varphi_{1}|_{n}

\displaystyle\mapsto\pi^{(1)}_{n+1,1}

(97a)

\displaystyle\frac{B(a_{1})}{2a_{1}}|\varphi_{1}|_{n}

\displaystyle\mapsto\pi^{(1)}_{n+1,-1}

(97b)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{La_{1}^{2}}|\varphi_{1}|_{n}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\pi}^{(0)}_{n+1,1}\ ,

(97c)

and, similarly, contributions from Eq. (
91b
) are to coefficients

\pi^{(2)}_{n+1,\pm 1}
and
\pi^{(0)}_{n+1,1}
,

\displaystyle\frac{A(a_{2})}{2a_{2}}|\varphi_{2}|_{n}

\displaystyle\mapsto\pi^{(2)}_{n+1,1}

(98a)

\displaystyle\frac{B(a_{2})}{2a_{2}}|\varphi_{2}|_{n}

\displaystyle\mapsto\pi^{(2)}_{n+1,-1}

(98b)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{La_{2}^{2}}|\varphi_{2}|_{n}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\pi}^{(0)}_{n+1,1}\ .

(98c)

)

(99d)

(99g)

From Eq. (

),

\displaystyle\frac{A(a_{1})}{2a_{1}}|\gamma_{1}|_{n}

(100a)

F.3
Contributions to
\rho_{n+1,m}

Contributions to
\rho_{n+1,m}
derive from Eq. (
94
)
and follow a different pattern compared to
\pi
and
\zeta
.
From Eq. (
94c
),

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\frac{A(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)})}{2}}{((A(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)}))/2)}{\frac{A(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)})}{2}}{((A(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)}))/2)}(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)}+\xi^{-1})[\eta]_{n,{m}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &{\rho}^{(0)}_{n+1,{m}+1}&\text{for }{m}=1,\ldots,M^{(0)}_{n}\end{aligned}

(102a)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\frac{B(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)})}{2}}{((B(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)}))/2)}{\frac{B(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)})}{2}}{((B(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)}))/2)}(p_{n,{m}}^{(0)}-\xi^{-1})[\eta]_{n,{m}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &{\rho}^{(0)}_{n+1,{m}-1}&\text{for }{m}=2,\ldots,M^{(0)}_{n}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\frac{A(p^{(1)}_{n,m})}{2}}{((A(p^{(1)}_{n,m}))/2)}{\frac{A(p^{(1)}_{n,m})}{2}}{((A(p^{(1)}_{n,m}))/2)}(p^{(1)}_{n,m}+\xi^{-1})[\eta]_{n,m}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &\rho^{(1)}_{n+1,m+1}&\text{for }m=-M^{(1)}_{n},\ldots,M^{(1)}_{n}\end{aligned}

(102c)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\frac{B(p^{(1)}_{n,m})}{2}}{((B(p^{(1)}_{n,m}))/2)}{\frac{B(p^{(1)}_{n,m})}{2}}{((B(p^{(1)}_{n,m}))/2)}(p^{(1)}_{n,m}-\xi^{-1})[\eta]_{n,m}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &\rho^{(1)}_{n+1,m-1}&\text{for }m=-M^{(1)}_{n},\ldots,M^{(1)}_{n}\end{aligned}

(102d)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\frac{A(p^{(2)}_{n,m})}{2}}{((A(p^{(2)}_{n,m}))/2)}{\frac{A(p^{(2)}_{n,m})}{2}}{((A(p^{(2)}_{n,m}))/2)}(p^{(2)}_{n,m}+\xi^{-1})[\eta]_{n,m}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &\rho^{(2)}_{n+1,m+1}&\text{for }m=-M^{(2)}_{n},\ldots,M^{(2)}_{n}\end{aligned}

(102e)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\frac{B(p^{(2)}_{n,m})}{2}}{((B(p^{(2)}_{n,m}))/2)}{\frac{B(p^{(2)}_{n,m})}{2}}{((B(p^{(2)}_{n,m}))/2)}(p^{(2)}_{n,m}-\xi^{-1})[\eta]_{n,m}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{aligned} &\rho^{(2)}_{n+1,m-1}&\text{for }m=-M^{(1)}_{n},\ldots,M^{(1)}_{n}\ .\end{aligned}

From Eq. (

),

\displaystyle-\mathchoice{\frac{A(a_{1})}{2}}{((A(a_{1}))/2)}{\frac{A(a_{1})}{2}}{((A(a_{1}))/2)}(a_{1}+\xi^{-1})|\eta_{1}|_{n}

\displaystyle\mapsto\rho^{(1)}_{n+1,1}

\displaystyle\mapsto\rho^{(1)}_{n+1,-1}\ .

\displaystyle-\mathchoice{\frac{A(a_{2})}{2}}{((A(a_{2}))/2)}{\frac{A(a_{2})}{2}}{((A(a_{2}))/2)}(a_{2}+\xi^{-1})|\eta_{2}|_{n}

(104a)

\displaystyle\mapsto\rho^{(2)}_{n+1,-1}\ .

(104b)

Appendix G
Iteration of
P
,
Q
and
R

This section contains the explicit form of the
iterative relations in Eqs. (
IV.3
), (
IV.3
) and (
IV.3
)
with coefficients in Eqs. (
32
), (
34
) and (
36
), in dimensionless
form.
Defining the dimensionless wavenumber
\Lambda_{j}=k_{j}\xi=2\pi j\bar{\xi}
, we have

\displaystyle P_{n+1}(\Lambda_{j})\Lambda_{j}^{2}=

\displaystyle-\Lambda_{j}\bar{\xi}\bar{\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{i}}{(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{i})^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\times\left\{\frac{\Lambda_{i}^{2}(\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}(2+\mathsf{Pe}))+\bar{\gamma}^{2}}{(\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma})(\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}(2+\mathsf{Pe}))}P_{n}(\Lambda_{i})+\frac{\bar{\gamma}}{\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}(2+\mathsf{Pe})}Q_{n}(\Lambda_{i})-\frac{\bar{\gamma}\sqrt{\mathsf{Pe}\bar{\gamma}}}{(\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma})(\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}(2+\mathsf{Pe}))}\xi R_{n}(\Lambda_{i})\right\}

(105a)

\displaystyle Q_{n+1}(\Lambda_{j})\Lambda_{j}^{2}=

\displaystyle-\Lambda_{j}\bar{\xi}\bar{\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{i}}{(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{i})^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\times\left\{\frac{\bar{\gamma}}{\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}(2+\mathsf{Pe})}P_{n}(\Lambda_{i})+\frac{\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}}{\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}(2+\mathsf{Pe})}Q_{n}(\Lambda_{i})-\frac{\sqrt{\mathsf{Pe}\bar{\gamma}}}{\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}(2+\mathsf{Pe})}\xi R_{n}(\Lambda_{i})\right\}

(105b)

\displaystyle\xi R_{n+1}(\Lambda_{j})\mathring{\imath}\Lambda_{j}=

\displaystyle-\Lambda_{j}\bar{\xi}\bar{\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathds{Z}\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{i})\mathring{\imath}\Lambda_{i}}{(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{i})^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\times\left\{\frac{\bar{\gamma}\sqrt{\mathsf{Pe}\bar{\gamma}}}{(\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma})(\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}(2+\mathsf{Pe}))}P_{n}(\Lambda_{i})+\frac{\sqrt{\mathsf{Pe}\bar{\gamma}}}{\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}(2+\mathsf{Pe})}Q_{n}(\Lambda_{i})+\frac{\Lambda_{i}^{2}+2\bar{\gamma}}{(\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma})(\Lambda_{i}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}(2+\mathsf{Pe}))}\xi R_{n}(\Lambda_{i})\right\}

(105c)

with base case in Eq. (
38
),

\displaystyle P_{1}(\Lambda_{j})=Q_{1}(\Lambda_{j})=

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathsf{D}_{x}}{2L}\frac{\bar{\nu}\bar{\xi}}{\Lambda_{j}^{2}+1}\ ,

(106a)

\displaystyle R_{1}(\Lambda_{j})=0\ .

(106b)

Writing the iterative evolution of
P
,
Q
and
R

in this form shows the role of each of them.
We find that
R_{n}
, the part of
\Psi_{n}
odd in
\Lambda_{j}
, is the one that
carries the clearest signature of activity:

R_{n}
is generated through
\mathsf{Pe}>0
at
n=2
and its effect in
P_{n+1}
and

Q_{n+1}
is proportional to
\sqrt{\mathsf{Pe}}
.
Meanwhile, the even part of the effective interaction vertices

\Phi_{n}
and
\Psi_{n}
,
P_{n}
and
Q_{n}
respectively,
play a similar role in each other’s evolution and even in that of
R_{n}
, Eq. (
105
).
Starting at
R_{1}=0
, the numerical value of
\xi R_{n}
is small compared to
P_{n}
and
Q_{n}
.
Thus,
P_{n}
and
Q_{n}
, which are identical to each other in the passive case, and alternate sign with
n
,
follow a similar pattern in the active case, albeit somewhat modified by a non-zero
\xi R_{n}
.
The contribution from
\xi R_{n}
effectively suppresses the amplitude of
P_{n}
and
Q_{n}
for odd
n
,
where
P_{n}
and
Q_{n}
are negative, while leaving their amplitude essentially unchanged for even
n
.

Appendix H
Compressibility factor

The compressibility factor
S_{1}=\mathchoice{\left\langle\cos(k_{1}(x_{1}-x_{2}))\right\rangle}{\langle\cos(k_{1}(x_{1}-x_{2}))\rangle}{\langle\cos(k_{1}(x_{1}-x_{2}))\rangle}{\langle\cos(k_{1}(x_{1}-x_{2}))\rangle}
defined in
Garcia-Millan et al. 2026
as the lowest Fourier mode of the
two-point correlation function, or the lowest non-zero mode of the structure factor

S_{j}=\mathchoice{\left\langle\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}\right\rangle}{\langle\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}\rangle}{\langle\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}\rangle}{\langle\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{j}(x_{1}-x_{2})\right)}\rangle}
,
can be calculated analytically following our iterative scheme detailed above.
Defining the factor
f=\sqrt{2+\mathsf{Pe}}=\sqrt{2\mathsf{D}_{\text{eff}}/\mathsf{D}_{x}}
, the compressibility factor is, up to second order in
\bar{\nu}=\nu/(\mathsf{D}_{x}\xi)
,

\displaystyle S_{1}=

\displaystyle-\bar{\nu}\bar{\xi}\frac{2\left(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}\right)\left(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+2\bar{\gamma}\right)+\mathsf{Pe}\bar{\gamma}\Lambda_{1}^{2}}{\left(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+1\right)\left(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}\right)\left(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}(2+\mathsf{Pe})\right)}

\displaystyle+\bar{\nu}^{2}\bar{\xi}\frac{1}{2(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+4)(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+\bar{\gamma})(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+\bar{\gamma}f^{2})(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}})^{2})(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}}f)^{2})}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\frac{(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+\bar{\gamma})(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+2\bar{\gamma})(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}})^{2})}{1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}}f}\left(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+1+6\bar{\gamma}+\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}}}{f}\left(2\Lambda_{1}^{2}+2\bar{\gamma}f^{2}+8-f^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\left(k_{1}^{4}\xi^{4}+\bar{\gamma}(1+f^{2})\Lambda_{1}^{2}+2\bar{\gamma}^{2}\right)\Bigg((\Lambda_{1}^{2}+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}})^{2})(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}}f)^{2})\left(-1+\frac{\mathsf{Pe}\bar{\gamma}^{2}}{(1-\bar{\gamma})(1-\bar{\gamma}f^{2})}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathsf{Pe}\frac{\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}}(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+4)}{1+\mathsf{Pe}}\left(\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}})^{2}}{f(1-\bar{\gamma}f^{2})}+\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}}f)^{2}}{1-\bar{\gamma}}\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathsf{Pe}\bar{\gamma}(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+2\bar{\gamma})}{(1+\mathsf{Pe})(1-\bar{\gamma})(1-\bar{\gamma}f^{2})}\Bigg(2(1+\mathsf{Pe})(2\bar{\gamma}-1)(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}})^{2})(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}}f)^{2})

\displaystyle+\mathsf{Pe}(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}}f)(1-\bar{\gamma})(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+4)(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}})^{2})+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}})(1-\bar{\gamma}f^{2})(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+4)(\Lambda_{1}^{2}+(1+\sqrt{\bar{\gamma}}f)^{2})\Bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\bar{\nu}^{3}\right)\ .

(107)
